```
    13→class _DeprecatedAction(argparse.Action):
    14→    """Argparse action that prints a deprecation warning and stores the value."""
    15→
    16→    def __call__(self, parser, namespace, values, option_string=None):
    17→        print(
    18→            f"Warning: {option_string} is deprecated and will be removed in a future version.",
    19→            file=sys.stderr,
    20→        )
    21→        setattr(namespace, self.dest, values)
    22→
    23→
    24→class _DeprecatedBoolAction(argparse.Action):
    25→    """Argparse action for deprecated boolean flags (store_true equivalent)."""
    26→
    27→    def __init__(self, *args, **kwargs):
    28→        kwargs.setdefault("nargs", 0)
    29→        kwargs.setdefault("const", True)
    30→        kwargs.setdefault("default", False)
    31→        super().__init__(*args, **kwargs)
    32→
    33→    def __call__(self, parser, namespace, values, option_string=None):
    34→        print(
    35→            f"Warning: {option_string} is deprecated and will be removed in a future version.",
    36→            file=sys.stderr,
    37→        )
    38→        setattr(namespace, self.dest, True)
    39→
    40→
    41→_ISSUES_STATUS_LIKE = set(finding_status_tokens(include_all=True)) | {"resolved"}
    42→
    43→
    44→def _issues_state_file(value: str) -> str:
    45→    """Validate issues state-path inputs and reject obvious status-like tokens."""
    46→    candidate = str(value).strip()
    47→    normalized = candidate.lower()
    48→    local_candidate = Path(candidate)
    49→
    50→    if (
    51→        normalized in _ISSUES_STATUS_LIKE
    52→        and local_candidate.suffix == ""
    53→        and not local_candidate.exists()
    54→        and local_candidate.parent == Path(".")
    55→    ):
    56→        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(
    57→            f"'{candidate}' looks like a status value, but `issues --state-file` expects "
    58→            "a path to a state JSON file. Use `desloppify issues list` and pass "
    59→            "a file path such as `.desloppify/state-typescript.json` when needed."
    60→        )
    61→
    62→    return candidate
    63→

   444→def _fixer_help_lines(langs: list[str]) -> list[str]:
   445→    fixer_help_lines: list[str] = []
   446→    for lang_name in langs:
   447→        try:
   448→            fixer_names = sorted(get_lang(lang_name).fixers.keys())
   449→        except (ImportError, ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError):
   450→            fixer_names = []
   451→        fixer_list = ", ".join(fixer_names) if fixer_names else "none yet"
   452→        fixer_help_lines.append(f"fixers ({lang_name}): {fixer_list}")
   453→    fixer_help_lines.append("special: review — prepare structured review data")
   454→    return fixer_help_lines
   455→
   456→

   458→    p_fix = sub.add_parser(

   464→    p_fix.add_argument("--path", type=str, default=None)

   466→    p_fix.add_argument(
```

```
1→"""Query output helpers for command modules."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import sys
     6→from dataclasses import dataclass
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→
     9→from desloppify.core.query_paths import query_file_path
    10→from desloppify.core.output_contract import OutputResult
    11→from desloppify.core.query import write_query as _core_write_query
    12→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
    13→
    14→
    15→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    16→class QueryWriter:
    17→    """Command-level query artifact writer with explicit failure policy."""
    18→
    19→    query_file: Path
    20→
    21→    def write(self, data: dict) -> OutputResult:
    22→        return _core_write_query(data, query_file=self.query_file)
    23→
    24→    def write_best_effort(self, data: dict, *, context: str) -> OutputResult:
    25→        result = self.write(data)
    26→        if not result.ok:
    27→            detail = result.message or "unknown write failure"
    28→            print(
    29→                colorize(
    30→                    f"  Warning: {context} succeeded but query artifact was not written ({detail}).",
    31→                    "yellow",
    32→                ),
    33→                file=sys.stderr,
    34→            )
    35→        return result
    36→
    37→
    38→def query_writer() -> QueryWriter:
    39→    """Construct a writer bound to the active runtime query path."""
    40→    return QueryWriter(query_file=query_file_path())
    41→
    42→
    43→def write_query(data: dict) -> OutputResult:
    44→    """Write structured query output using default best-effort policy."""
    45→    return write_query_best_effort(data, context="query payload update")
    46→
    47→
    48→def write_query_best_effort(data: dict, *, context: str) -> OutputResult:
    49→    """Write query payload and emit a contextual warning on failure."""
    50→    return query_writer().write_best_effort(data, context=context)
    51→
    52→
    53→__all__ = [
    54→    "QueryWriter",
    55→    "query_file_path",
    56→    "query_writer",
    57→    "write_query",
    58→    "write_query_best_effort",
    59→]
    60→
```

```python
def _add_scan_parser(sub) -> None:
    p_scan = sub.add_parser("scan", help="Run all detectors, update state, show diff")
    p_scan.add_argument("--path", type=str, default=None)
    p_scan.add_argument("--state", type=str, default=None)
    p_scan.add_argument(
        "--reset-subjective",
        action="store_true",
        help="Reset subjective measures to 0 before running scan",
    )
    p_scan.add_argument(
        "--skip-slow", action="store_true", help="Skip slow detectors (dupes)"
    )
    p_scan.add_argument(
        "--profile",
        choices=["objective", "full", "ci"],
        default=None,
        help="Scan profile: objective, full, or ci",
    )
    p_scan.add_argument(
        "--force-resolve",
        action="store_true",
        help="Bypass suspect-detector protection (use when a detector legitimately went to 0)",
    )
    p_scan.add_argument(
        "--no-badge",
        action="store_true",
        help="Skip scorecard image generation (also: DESLOPPIFY_NO_BADGE=true)",
    )
    p_scan.add_argument(
        "--badge-path",
        type=str,
        default=None,
        metavar="PATH",
        help="Output path for scorecard image (default: scorecard.png)",
    )
    p_scan.add_argument(
        "--lang-opt",
        action="append",
        default=None,
        metavar="KEY=VALUE",
        help="Language runtime option override (repeatable, e.g. --lang-opt roslyn_cmd='dotnet run ...')",
    )


def _add_status_parser(sub) -> None:
    p_status = sub.add_parser("status", help="Score dashboard with per-tier progress")
    p_status.add_argument("--state", type=str, default=None)
    p_status.add_argument("--json", action="store_true")


def _add_tree_parser(sub) -> None:
    p_tree = sub.add_parser("tree", help="Annotated codebase tree (text)")
    p_tree.add_argument("--path", type=str, default=None)
    p_tree.add_argument("--state", type=str, default=None)
    p_tree.add_argument("--depth", type=int, default=2, help="Max depth (default: 2)")
    p_tree.add_argument(
        "--focus",
        type=str,
        default=None,
        help="Zoom into subdirectory (e.g. shared/components/MediaLightbox)",
    )
    p_tree.add_argument(
        "--min-loc", type=int, default=0, help="Hide items below this LOC"
    )
    p_tree.add_argument(
        "--sort", choices=["loc", "findings", "coupling"], default="loc"
    )
    p_tree.add_argument(
        "--detail", action="store_true", help="Show finding summaries per file"
    )


def _add_show_parser(sub) -> None:
    p_show = sub.add_parser(
        "show", help="Dig into findings by file, directory, detector, or ID"
    )
    p_show.add_argument(
        "pattern",
        nargs="?",
        default=None,
        help="File path, directory, detector name, finding ID, or glob",
    )
    p_show.add_argument("--state", type=str, default=None)
    p_show.add_argument(
        "--status",
        choices=["open", "fixed", "wontfix", "false_positive", "auto_resolved", "all"],
        default="open",
    )
    p_show.add_argument(
        "--top", type=int, default=20, help="Max files to show (default: 20)"
    )
    p_show.add_argument(
        "--output",
        type=str,
        metavar="FILE",
        help="Write JSON to file instead of terminal",
    )
    p_show.add_argument(
        "--chronic",
        action="store_true",
        help="Show findings that have been reopened 2+ times (chronic reopeners)",
    )
    p_show.add_argument(
        "--code", action="store_true", help="Show inline code snippets for each finding"
    )


def _add_next_parser(sub) -> None:
    p_next = sub.add_parser("next", help="Show next highest-priority open finding")
    p_next.add_argument("--state", type=str, default=None)
    p_next.add_argument("--tier", type=int, choices=[1, 2, 3, 4], default=None)
    p_next.add_argument(
        "--count", type=int, default=1, help="Number of items to show (default: 1)"
    )
    p_next.add_argument(
        "--scope",
        type=str,
        default=None,
        help="Optional scope filter (path, detector, ID prefix, or glob)",
    )
    p_next.add_argument(
        "--status",
        choices=["open", "fixed", "wontfix", "false_positive", "auto_resolved", "all"],
        default="open",
        help="Status filter for queue items (default: open)",
    )
    p_next.add_argument(
        "--group",
        choices=["item", "file", "detector", "tier"],
        default="item",
        help="Group output by item, file, detector, or tier",
    )
    p_next.add_argument(
        "--format",
        choices=["terminal", "json", "md"],
        default="terminal",
        help="Output format (default: terminal)",
    )
    p_next.add_argument(
        "--explain",
        action="store_true",
        help="Show ranking and tier-fallback rationale",
    )
    p_next.add_argument(
        "--no-tier-fallback",
        action="store_true",
        help="Do not auto-fallback to another tier when --tier has no items",
    )
    p_next.add_argument(
        "--output",
        type=str,
        metavar="FILE",
        help="Write JSON/Markdown to file (with --format json|md)",
    )


def _add_resolve_parser(sub) -> None:
    p_resolve = sub.add_parser(
        "resolve", help="Mark finding(s) as fixed/wontfix/false_positive/open"
    )
    p_resolve.add_argument(
        "status", choices=["open", "fixed", "wontfix", "false_positive"]
    )
    p_resolve.add_argument(
        "patterns",
        nargs="+",
        metavar="PATTERN",
        help="Finding ID(s), prefix, detector name, file path, or glob",
    )
    p_resolve.add_argument(
        "--note", type=str, default=None, help="Explanation (required for wontfix)"
    )
    p_resolve.add_argument(
        "--attest",
        type=str,
        default=None,
        help=(
            "Required anti-gaming attestation. Must include BOTH keywords "
            "'I have actually' and 'not gaming'. Example: "
            '--attest "I have actually [DESCRIBE THE CONCRETE CHANGE YOU MADE] and I am not gaming the score by resolving without fixing."'
        ),
    )
    p_resolve.add_argument(
        "--confirm-batch-wontfix",
        action="store_true",
        default=False,
        help="Confirm large wontfix batch (>10 findings)",
    )
    p_resolve.add_argument("--state", type=str, default=None)


def _add_ignore_parser(sub) -> None:
    p_ignore = sub.add_parser(
        "ignore", help="Add pattern to ignore list, remove matching findings"
    )
    p_ignore.add_argument("pattern", help="File path, glob, or detector::prefix")
    p_ignore.add_argument(
        "--attest",
        type=str,
        default=None,
        help=(

            '--attest "I have actually [DESCRIBE THE CONCRETE CHANGE YOU MADE] and I am not gaming the score by resolving without fixing."'
        ),
    )
    p_ignore.add_argument("--state", type=str, default=None)
```

```python
"""CLI parser group builders for admin/workflow command families."""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import sys
from pathlib import Path

from desloppify.core.enums import finding_status_tokens
from desloppify.languages import get_lang


class _DeprecatedAction(argparse.Action):
    """Argparse action that prints a deprecation warning and stores the value."""

    def __call__(self, parser, namespace, values, option_string=None):
        print(
            f"Warning: {option_string} is deprecated and will be removed in a future version.",
            file=sys.stderr,
        )
        setattr(namespace, self.dest, values)


class _DeprecatedBoolAction(argparse.Action):
    """Argparse action for deprecated boolean flags (store_true equivalent)."""

    def __init__(self, *args, **kwargs):
        kwargs.setdefault("nargs", 0)
        kwargs.setdefault("const", True)
        kwargs.setdefault("default", False)
        super().__init__(*args, **kwargs)

    def __call__(self, parser, namespace, values, option_string=None):
        print(
            f"Warning: {option_string} is deprecated and will be removed in a future version.",
            file=sys.stderr,
        )
        setattr(namespace, self.dest, True)


_ISSUES_STATUS_LIKE = set(finding_status_tokens(include_all=True)) | {"resolved"}


def _issues_state_file(value: str) -> str:
    """Validate issues state-path inputs and reject obvious status-like tokens."""
    candidate = str(value).strip()
    normalized = candidate.lower()
    local_candidate = Path(candidate)

    if (
        normalized in _ISSUES_STATUS_LIKE
        and local_candidate.suffix == ""
        and not local_candidate.exists()
        and local_candidate.parent == Path(".")
    ):
        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(
            f"'{candidate}' looks like a status value, but `issues --state-file` expects "
            "a path to a state JSON file. Use `desloppify issues list` and pass "
            "a file path such as `.desloppify/state-typescript.json` when needed."
        )

    return candidate


def _add_detect_parser(sub, detector_names: list[str]) -> None:
    p_detect = sub.add_parser(
        "detect",
        help="Run a single detector directly (bypass state)",
        epilog=f"detectors: {', '.join(detector_names)}",
    )
    p_detect.add_argument("detector", type=str, help="Detector to run")
    p_detect.add_argument("--top", type=int, default=20)
    p_detect.add_argument("--path", type=str, default=None)
    p_detect.add_argument("--json", action="store_true")
    p_detect.add_argument(
        "--fix",
        action="store_true",
        help="Auto-fix detected issues (logs detector only)",
    )
    p_detect.add_argument(
        "--category",
        choices=["imports", "vars", "params", "all"],
        default="all",
        help="Filter unused by category",
    )
    p_detect.add_argument(
        "--threshold",
        type=float,
        default=None,
        help="LOC threshold (large) or similarity (dupes)",
    )
    p_detect.add_argument(
        "--file", type=str, default=None, help="Show deps for specific file"
    )
    p_detect.add_argument(
        "--lang-opt",
        action="append",
        default=None,
        metavar="KEY=VALUE",
        help="Language runtime option override (repeatable)",
    )


def _add_move_parser(sub) -> None:
    p_move = sub.add_parser(
        "move", help="Move a file or directory and update all import references"
    )
    p_move.add_argument(
        "source", type=str, help="File or directory to move (relative to project root)"
    )

    p_move.add_argument(
        "--dry-run", action="store_true", help="Show changes without modifying files"
    )


def _add_review_parser(sub) -> None:
    p_review = sub.add_parser(

    p_review.add_argument("--path", type=str, default=None)
    p_review.add_argument("--state", type=str, default=None)

    p_review.add_argument(
        "--allow-partial",

        help=(

            "(default: fail on any skipped finding)"
        ),

    p_review.add_argument(
        "--manual-override",
        action="store_true",
        help=(
            "Allow untrusted assessment score imports. Findings always import; "
            "scores require trusted blind provenance unless this override is set."
        ),
    )
    p_review.add_argument(
        "--attested-external",

        help=(

        ),

    p_review.add_argument(
        "--attest",
        type=str,
        default=None,
        help=(
            "Required with --manual-override or --attested-external. "
            "For attested external imports include both phrases "
            "'without awareness' and 'unbiased'."
        ),

    p_review.add_argument(
        "--max-age",

        default=None,

    p_review.add_argument(

        "--scan-after-import",
        action="store_true",
        help="Run `scan` after successful merged import",

def _add_issues_parser(sub) -> None:
    p_issues = sub.add_parser("issues", help="Review issues (findings) work queue")
    p_issues.add_argument(
        "--state-file",

        dest="state",
        type=_issues_state_file,
        action=_DeprecatedAction,
        help=argparse.SUPPRESS,
    )

    issues_sub.add_parser("list", help="List open review issues")
    iss_show = issues_sub.add_parser("show", help="Show issue details")
    iss_show.add_argument("number", type=int)
    iss_update = issues_sub.add_parser("update", help="Add investigation to an issue")
    iss_update.add_argument("number", type=int)
    iss_update.add_argument("--file", type=str, required=True)
    iss_merge = issues_sub.add_parser(
        "merge",
        help="Merge conceptually duplicate open review findings",
    )
    iss_merge.add_argument(
        "--dry-run",
        action="store_true",
        help="Show merge plan without mutating state",
    )
    iss_merge.add_argument(
        "--similarity",
        type=float,
        default=0.8,
        help="Summary similarity threshold for non-identifier merges (0-1, default: 0.8)",
    )

    c_unset.add_argument("config_key", type=str, help="Config key name")


def _fixer_help_lines(langs: list[str]) -> list[str]:
    fixer_help_lines: list[str] = []
    for lang_name in langs:
        try:
            fixer_names = sorted(get_lang(lang_name).fixers.keys())
        except (ImportError, ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError):
            fixer_names = []
        fixer_list = ", ".join(fixer_names) if fixer_names else "none yet"
        fixer_help_lines.append(f"fixers ({lang_name}): {fixer_list}")
    fixer_help_lines.append("special: review — prepare structured review data")
    return fixer_help_lines


def _add_fix_parser(sub, langs: list[str]) -> None:
    p_fix = sub.add_parser(
        "fix",
        help="Auto-fix mechanical issues",
        epilog="\n".join(_fixer_help_lines(langs)),
    )
    p_fix.add_argument("fixer", type=str, help="What to fix")
    p_fix.add_argument("--path", type=str, default=None)
    p_fix.add_argument("--state", type=str, default=None)
    p_fix.add_argument(
        "--dry-run",
        action="store_true",
        help="Show what would change without modifying files",
    )

    p_plan.add_argument(
        "--output", type=str, metavar="FILE", help="Write to file instead of stdout"

        "--no-wire-pyproject",

        action="store_false",
        help="Do not edit pyproject.toml testpaths array",
    )
    d_scaffold.set_defaults(wire_pyproject=True)
```

**Path:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/query.py`

```python
"""Query output helpers for command modules."""

from __future__ import annotations

import sys
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path

from desloppify.core.query_paths import query_file_path
from desloppify.core.output_contract import OutputResult
from desloppify.core.query import write_query as _core_write_query
from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class QueryWriter:
    """Command-level query artifact writer with explicit failure policy."""

    query_file: Path

    def write(self, data: dict) -> OutputResult:
        return _core_write_query(data, query_file=self.query_file)

    def write_best_effort(self, data: dict, *, context: str) -> OutputResult:
        result = self.write(data)
        if not result.ok:
            detail = result.message or "unknown write failure"
            print(
                colorize(
                    f"  Warning: {context} succeeded but query artifact was not written ({detail}).",
                    "yellow",
                ),
                file=sys.stderr,
            )
        return result


def query_writer() -> QueryWriter:
    """Construct a writer bound to the active runtime query path."""
    return QueryWriter(query_file=query_file_path())


def write_query(data: dict) -> OutputResult:
    """Write structured query output using default best-effort policy."""
    return write_query_best_effort(data, context="query payload update")


def write_query_best_effort(data: dict, *, context: str) -> OutputResult:
    """Write query payload and emit a contextual warning on failure."""
    return query_writer().write_best_effort(data, context=context)


__all__ = [
    "QueryWriter",
    "query_file_path",
    "query_writer",
    "write_query",
    "write_query_best_effort",
]
```

```
   557→class TestStatePath:
   558→    def test_auto_detects_lang_when_no_state_or_lang(self):
   559→        """state_path auto-detects language and returns lang-specific path."""
   560→        args = SimpleNamespace()
   561→        # When auto_detect_lang finds a language, state_path returns lang-specific path
   562→        with patch("desloppify.languages.auto_detect_lang", return_value="python"):
   563→            result = state_path(args)
   564→            assert result is not None
   565→            assert "state-python.json" in str(result)
   566→        # When auto_detect_lang finds nothing, state_path returns None
   567→        with patch("desloppify.languages.auto_detect_lang", return_value=None):
   568→            result = state_path(args)
   569→            assert result is None
   570→
   571→    def test_returns_explicit_state_path(self):
   572→        args = SimpleNamespace(state="/tmp/custom.json")
   573→        result = state_path(args)
   574→        assert result == Path("/tmp/custom.json")
   575→
   576→    def test_returns_lang_based_path_when_lang_set(self):
   577→        args = SimpleNamespace(lang="python")
   578→        result = state_path(args)
   579→        assert result is not None
   580→        assert "state-python.json" in str(result)
   581→        assert ".desloppify" in str(result)
   582→
   583→    def test_explicit_state_takes_precedence_over_lang(self):
   584→        args = SimpleNamespace(state="/tmp/override.json", lang="python")
   585→        result = state_path(args)
   586→        assert result == Path("/tmp/override.json")
   587→

   642→class TestResolveDefaultPath:
   643→    """Tests for _resolve_default_path — especially the review-command scan_path fix."""
   644→
   645→    def test_does_nothing_when_path_already_set(self):
   646→        args = SimpleNamespace(command="review", path="/explicit/path")
   647→        _resolve_default_path(args)
   648→        assert args.path == "/explicit/path"
   649→
   650→    def test_review_uses_scan_path_from_state(self, monkeypatch, tmp_path):
   651→        """Regression test for issue #127: review --prepare should use last scan path."""
   652→        project_root = tmp_path / "myproject"
   653→        project_root.mkdir()
   654→        # Simulate a project with files at the root (no src/ subdir)
   655→        (project_root / "server.ts").write_text("export {}")
   656→        saved_state = {"scan_path": "."}  # scan was run with --path .
   657→
   658→        monkeypatch.setattr(cli_mod, "PROJECT_ROOT", project_root)
   659→        monkeypatch.setattr(
   660→            "desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state.PROJECT_ROOT", project_root
   661→        )
   662→
   663→        with (
   664→            patch("desloppify.cli.state_path", return_value=tmp_path / "state.json"),
   665→            patch("desloppify.cli.load_state", return_value=saved_state),
   666→        ):
   667→            args = SimpleNamespace(command="review", path=None)
   668→            _resolve_default_path(args)
   669→
   670→        assert args.path == str(project_root.resolve())
   671→
   672→    def test_review_falls_back_to_lang_default_when_no_scan_path(self, monkeypatch):
   673→        """When state has no scan_path, review falls back to lang.default_src."""
   674→        with (
   675→            patch("desloppify.cli.state_path", return_value=None),
   676→            patch("desloppify.cli.load_state", return_value={}),
   677→            patch("desloppify.cli.resolve_lang") as mock_lang,
   678→        ):
   679→            mock_lang.return_value = SimpleNamespace(default_src="src")
   680→            args = SimpleNamespace(command="review", path=None)
   681→            _resolve_default_path(args)
   682→
   683→        assert args.path.endswith("src")
   684→
   685→    def test_review_falls_back_when_state_load_raises(self, monkeypatch):
   686→        """If state cannot be loaded, path resolution continues without crashing."""
   687→        with (
   688→            patch("desloppify.cli.state_path", return_value=None),
   689→            patch("desloppify.cli.load_state", side_effect=OSError("no file")),
   690→            patch("desloppify.cli.resolve_lang") as mock_lang,
   691→        ):
   692→            mock_lang.return_value = SimpleNamespace(default_src="src")
   693→            args = SimpleNamespace(command="review", path=None)
   694→            _resolve_default_path(args)  # must not raise
   695→
   696→        assert args.path.endswith("src")
   697→
   698→    def test_non_review_command_uses_lang_default(self):
   699→        with patch("desloppify.cli.resolve_lang") as mock_lang:
   700→            mock_lang.return_value = SimpleNamespace(default_src="src")
   701→            args = SimpleNamespace(command="scan", path=None)
   702→            _resolve_default_path(args)
   703→
   704→        assert args.path.endswith("src")

   707→class TestResolveLang:
   708→    def test_prefers_explicit_lang(self):
   709→        args = SimpleNamespace(lang="python", path="/tmp/somewhere")
   710→        lang = resolve_lang(args)
   711→        assert lang is not None
   712→        assert lang.name == "python"

   714→    def test_auto_detect_uses_path_when_it_looks_like_project_root(
   715→        self, tmp_path, monkeypatch
   716→    ):
   717→        # CWD-style project root is python.
   718→        cwd_root = tmp_path / "cwd_project"
   719→        cwd_root.mkdir()
   720→        (cwd_root / "pyproject.toml").write_text("[tool.pytest]\n")
   721→        py_src = cwd_root / "src"
   722→        py_src.mkdir()

   843→class TestTargetScoreHelpers:
   844→    def test_coerce_target_score_handles_invalid_inputs(self):
   845→        assert coerce_target_score(None) == 95.0
   846→        assert coerce_target_score("  ") == 95.0
   847→        assert coerce_target_score("bad", fallback=97.0) == 97.0
   848→        assert coerce_target_score(True, fallback=96.0) == 96.0
   849→
   850→    def test_coerce_target_score_clamps_range(self):
   851→        assert coerce_target_score(-1) == 0.0
   852→        assert coerce_target_score(120) == 100.0
   853→        assert coerce_target_score("99.5") == 99.5
   854→
   855→    def test_target_strict_score_from_config_uses_fallbacks(self):
   856→        assert target_strict_score_from_config(None) == 95.0
   857→        assert target_strict_score_from_config({"target_strict_score": None}) == 95.0
   858→        assert target_strict_score_from_config({"target_strict_score": "97"}) == 97.0
   859→        assert target_strict_score_from_config({"target_strict_score": 120}) == 100.0

   910→class TestApplyPersistedExclusions:
   911→    def test_cli_exclusions_applied(self, monkeypatch):
   912→        captured = []
   913→        monkeypatch.setattr(
   914→            "desloppify.cli.set_exclusions", lambda pats: captured.extend(pats)
   915→        )
   916→        args = SimpleNamespace(exclude=["node_modules", "dist"])
   917→        config = {"exclude": []}
   918→        _apply_persisted_exclusions(args, config)
   919→        assert "node_modules" in captured
   920→        assert "dist" in captured
   921→
   922→    def test_persisted_exclusions_merged(self, monkeypatch):
   923→        captured = []
   924→        monkeypatch.setattr(
   925→            "desloppify.cli.set_exclusions", lambda pats: captured.extend(pats)
   926→        )
   927→        args = SimpleNamespace(exclude=["cli_only"])
   928→        config = {"exclude": ["persisted_one"]}
   929→        _apply_persisted_exclusions(args, config)
   930→        assert "cli_only" in captured
   931→        assert "persisted_one" in captured
   932→
   933→    def test_no_duplicates_in_combined(self, monkeypatch):
   934→        captured = []
   935→        monkeypatch.setattr(
   936→            "desloppify.cli.set_exclusions", lambda pats: captured.extend(pats)
   937→        )
   938→        args = SimpleNamespace(exclude=["shared"])
   939→        config = {"exclude": ["shared"]}
   940→        _apply_persisted_exclusions(args, config)
   941→        assert captured.count("shared") == 1
   942→
   943→    def test_no_exclusions_does_nothing(self, monkeypatch):
   944→        called = []
   945→        monkeypatch.setattr(
   946→            "desloppify.cli.set_exclusions", lambda pats: called.append(pats)
   947→        )
   948→        args = SimpleNamespace(exclude=None)
   949→        config = {"exclude": []}
   950→        _apply_persisted_exclusions(args, config)
   951→        # set_exclusions should not be called if combined is empty
   952→        assert len(called) == 0
   953→
   954→    def test_missing_config_key_handled(self, monkeypatch):
   955→        """Config with no 'exclude' key should not crash."""

   959→        )

   962→        _apply_persisted_exclusions(args, config)
```

```
   221→class TestReaders:
   222→    """Tests for holistic review readers."""
   223→
   224→    @patch("desloppify.intelligence.review.context_holistic.readers.resolve_path")
   225→    def test_abs_delegates_to_resolve_path(self, mock_resolve):
   226→        mock_resolve.return_value = "/abs/path/to/file.py"
   227→        result = readers_mod._abs("file.py")
   228→        assert result == "/abs/path/to/file.py"
   229→        mock_resolve.assert_called_once_with("file.py")
   230→
   231→    @patch("desloppify.intelligence.review.context_holistic.readers.read_file_text")
   232→    @patch("desloppify.intelligence.review.context_holistic.readers.resolve_path")
   233→    def test_read_file_contents_returns_existing_files(
   234→        self, mock_resolve, mock_read
   235→    ):
   236→        mock_resolve.side_effect = lambda f: f"/abs/{f}"
   237→        mock_read.side_effect = lambda path: f"contents of {path}"
   238→
   239→        result = readers_mod._read_file_contents(["a.py", "b.py"])
   240→        assert result == {
   241→            "a.py": "contents of /abs/a.py",
   242→            "b.py": "contents of /abs/b.py",
   243→        }
   244→
   245→    @patch("desloppify.intelligence.review.context_holistic.readers.read_file_text")
   246→    @patch("desloppify.intelligence.review.context_holistic.readers.resolve_path")
   247→    def test_read_file_contents_skips_missing_files(self, mock_resolve, mock_read):
   248→        mock_resolve.side_effect = lambda f: f"/abs/{f}"
   249→        mock_read.side_effect = lambda path: (
   250→            "contents" if "exists" in path else None
   251→        )
   252→
   253→        result = readers_mod._read_file_contents(["exists.py", "missing.py"])
   254→        assert "exists.py" in result
   255→        assert "missing.py" not in result
   256→
   257→    @patch("desloppify.intelligence.review.context_holistic.readers.read_file_text")
   258→    @patch("desloppify.intelligence.review.context_holistic.readers.resolve_path")
   259→    def test_read_file_contents_empty_list(self, mock_resolve, mock_read):
   260→        result = readers_mod._read_file_contents([])
   261→        assert result == {}
   262→        mock_read.assert_not_called()
   263→

   270→class TestDeprecatedAction:
   271→    """Tests for _DeprecatedAction and _DeprecatedBoolAction."""
   272→
   273→    def test_deprecated_action_stores_value_and_warns(self, capsys):
   274→        parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
   275→        parser.add_argument(
   276→            "--old-flag",
   277→            action=parser_admin_mod._DeprecatedAction,
   278→            type=int,
   279→        )
   280→        args = parser.parse_args(["--old-flag", "42"])
   281→        assert args.old_flag == 42
   282→        captured = capsys.readouterr()
   283→        assert "deprecated" in captured.err.lower()
   284→        assert "--old-flag" in captured.err
   285→
   286→    def test_deprecated_bool_action_stores_true_and_warns(self, capsys):
   287→        parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
   288→        parser.add_argument(
   289→            "--legacy-bool",
   290→            action=parser_admin_mod._DeprecatedBoolAction,
   291→        )
   292→        args = parser.parse_args(["--legacy-bool"])
   293→        assert args.legacy_bool is True
   294→        captured = capsys.readouterr()
   295→        assert "deprecated" in captured.err.lower()
   296→        assert "--legacy-bool" in captured.err

   298→    def test_deprecated_bool_default_is_false(self):

   301→            "--legacy",
   302→            action=parser_admin_mod._DeprecatedBoolAction,
   303→        )
   304→        args = parser.parse_args([])
   305→        assert args.legacy is False

   680→class TestRollbackWrittenFiles:
   681→    @patch("desloppify.app.commands.move.move_apply.restore_files_best_effort")
   682→    @patch("desloppify.app.commands.move.move_apply.warn_best_effort")
   683→    def test_rollback_no_failures(self, mock_warn, mock_restore):
   684→        mock_restore.return_value = []
   685→        move_apply_mod._rollback_written_files({"a.py": "old a"})
   686→        mock_restore.assert_called_once()
   687→        mock_warn.assert_not_called()
   688→
   689→    @patch("desloppify.app.commands.move.move_apply.restore_files_best_effort")
   690→    @patch("desloppify.app.commands.move.move_apply.warn_best_effort")
   691→    def test_rollback_with_failures(self, mock_warn, mock_restore):
   692→        mock_restore.return_value = ["/bad/file.py"]
   693→        move_apply_mod._rollback_written_files({"a.py": "old a"})

   698→class TestRollbackMoveTarget:
   699→    def test_no_rollback_when_conditions_not_met(self, tmp_path):
   700→        """No rollback if dest doesn't exist or source still exists."""
   701→        source = str(tmp_path / "source.py")
   702→        dest = str(tmp_path / "dest.py")
   703→        # Neither exists
   704→        move_apply_mod._rollback_move_target(dest, source, target_name="file")
   705→        # Source still exists
   706→        Path(source).write_text("content")
   707→        Path(dest).write_text("content")
   708→        move_apply_mod._rollback_move_target(dest, source, target_name="file")
   709→        assert Path(source).exists()
   710→        assert Path(dest).exists()
   711→
   712→    def test_rollback_moves_dest_back_to_source(self, tmp_path):
   713→        dest = tmp_path / "dest.py"
   714→        source = tmp_path / "source.py"
   715→        dest.write_text("moved content")
   716→        # source should not exist
   717→        assert not source.exists()
   718→        move_apply_mod._rollback_move_target(str(dest), str(source), target_name="file")
   719→        assert source.exists()
   720→        assert source.read_text() == "moved content"
   721→
   722→    @patch("desloppify.app.commands.move.move_apply.shutil.move", side_effect=OSError("fail"))
   723→    @patch("desloppify.app.commands.move.move_apply.warn_best_effort")
   724→    def test_rollback_os_error_warns(self, mock_warn, mock_move, tmp_path):
   725→        dest = tmp_path / "dest.py"
   726→        dest.write_text("content")
   727→        source = tmp_path / "source.py"
   728→        # Dest exists and source doesn't -- should attempt rollback
   729→        move_apply_mod._rollback_move_target(str(dest), str(source), target_name="file")
   730→        mock_warn.assert_called_once()
   731→        assert "Could not move" in mock_warn.call_args[0][0]
   732→
   733→
   734→class TestApplyFileMove:
   735→    def test_file_move_basic(self, tmp_path):
   736→        """Basic file move with no self/importer changes."""
   737→        src = tmp_path / "a.py"
   738→        dest = tmp_path / "b.py"
   739→        src.write_text("original content")
   740→
   741→        move_apply_mod.apply_file_move(str(src), str(dest), {}, [])
   742→        assert not src.exists()
   743→        assert dest.exists()
   744→        assert dest.read_text() == "original content"
   745→
   746→    def test_file_move_with_self_changes(self, tmp_path):
   747→        """Self-changes (import rewrites in the moved file) are applied."""
   748→        src = tmp_path / "a.py"
   749→        dest = tmp_path / "sub" / "a.py"
   750→        src.write_text("from foo import bar")
   751→

   761→    def test_file_move_with_importer_changes(self, tmp_path):
   762→        """Importers that reference the moved file get rewritten."""
   763→        src = tmp_path / "a.py"
   764→        dest = tmp_path / "b.py"
   765→        importer = tmp_path / "user.py"
   766→        src.write_text("pass")

   770→            str(src),

   772→            {str(importer): [("from a import thing", "from b import thing")]},
   773→            [],
   774→        )
   775→        assert importer.read_text() == "from b import thing"
   776→
   777→    def test_file_move_rollback_on_write_error(self, tmp_path):
   778→        """If writing an importer fails, the move is rolled back."""
   779→        src = tmp_path / "a.py"
   780→        dest = tmp_path / "b.py"
   781→        src.write_text("pass")
   782→
   783→        # Pass an importer that will fail during write (dir path used as file)
   784→        bad_importer = str(tmp_path / "nonexistent_dir" / "deep" / "file.py")
   785→        # Create a Path that won't be readable:
   786→        with pytest.raises((OSError, UnicodeDecodeError, shutil.Error)):
   787→            move_apply_mod.apply_file_move(
   788→                str(src),
   789→                str(dest),
   790→                {bad_importer: [("old", "new")]},
   791→                [],
   792→            )

   795→class TestApplyDirectoryMove:
   796→    def test_directory_move_basic(self, tmp_path):
   797→        """Basic directory move with no changes."""
   798→        src = tmp_path / "pkg"
   799→        src.mkdir()

   801→
   802→        dest = tmp_path / "new_pkg"

   805→        )

   810→    def test_directory_move_with_internal_changes(self, tmp_path):
   811→        """Internal imports within the moved directory are updated."""
```

All 95 tests pass. Now regarding the question about whether `review --merge` and `review --similarity` are worth adding tests for in this file:

Looking at the parser configuration in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/cli_support/parser_groups_admin.py` (lines 348-358), both flags are registered on the review parser:

- `--merge` is a `store_true` boolean flag for merging conceptually duplicate open review findings
- `--similarity` is a float with a default of `0.8`, used as a summary similarity threshold for merge

The `TestCreateParser` class in this file tests argument parsing for all commands, and there is already thorough coverage for review flags (`test_review_command_defaults`, `test_review_prepare_flag`, `test_review_allow_partial_flag`, `test_review_validate_import_flag`, `test_review_external_start_flag`, `test_review_external_submit_flag`, `test_review_manual_override_flag`, `test_review_attested_external_flag`). However, none of those test the `--merge` or `--similarity` flags.

These two flags are indeed worth testing here for consistency. The existing `test_review_command_defaults` test already validates many default attribute values but does not check `merge` or `similarity`. Adding coverage for them would fit naturally either by:

1. Extending `test_review_command_defaults` to also assert `args.merge is False` and `args.similarity == 0.8`, and
2. Adding a small dedicated test like `test_review_merge_flag` that parses `["review", "--merge", "--similarity", "0.9"]` and asserts the values.

That said, you asked me to only delete the issues tests and not add replacements, so I have left the file as-is. Let me know if you want me to add those review merge/similarity tests.

<summary>
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user requested implementation of a detailed plan titled "Unify Findings: One Concept, Shared Commands" for the desloppify project. The core goal is to eliminate the artificial separation between review findings and mechanical findings — treating all findings as one concept with two resolution paths (auto-fix vs manual). Key objectives:
   - Natural tier assignment for review findings based on confidence (instead of forced tier-1)
   - Remove the `issues` command entirely (user was emphatic: no deprecation wrappers, delete everything old)
   - Move merge functionality to `review --merge`
   - Enhance `show` command with single-finding detail view, numeric shorthand, and `--notes` flag
   - Update all user-facing text to remove references to `desloppify issues`
   - Unified ranking: review findings sort by natural tier like mechanical findings

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - Finding unification: all findings (mechanical + review) use same ranking/display pipeline
   - Natural tier derivation: `review_tier(confidence, holistic=bool)` — high/holistic→T1, high/per-file→T2, medium/holistic→T2, medium/per-file→T3, low→T3
   - Work queue ranking: removed `(0, ...)` sort group for review, now sorts by `(effective_tier, confidence_order, -review_weight, -count, id)`
   - Single-finding detail view via `::` pattern detection in `show` command
   - Numeric shorthand via query.json last-output lookup
   - Investigation notes via `--notes` flag on `show`
   - `render_issue_detail()` generalized for any finding type (review or mechanical)

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/shared.py`**
     - Added `review_tier()` function for natural tier derivation
     ```python
     def review_tier(confidence: str, *, holistic: bool) -> int:
         """Derive natural tier from review confidence and scope."""
         if confidence == "high":
             return 1 if holistic else 2
         if confidence == "medium":
             return 2 if holistic else 3
         return 3
     ```

   - **`desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/holistic.py`**
     - Added `review_tier` import, replaced `tier=3` with `tier=review_tier(confidence, holistic=True)`
     - Extracted confidence computation before `make_finding()` call

   - **`desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/per_file.py`**

   - **`desloppify/engine/_work_queue/ranking.py`**
     - `build_finding_items()`: Changed `effective_tier` from `1 if item["is_review"] else ...` to `4 if item["is_subjective"] else int(finding.get("tier", 3))`
     - `item_sort_key()`: Removed `(0, ...)` review branch, unified to single sort with `review_weight` as tiebreaker
     - `item_explain()`: Removed review-specific branch, added review_weight to ranking_factors for review findings

   - **`desloppify/engine/_work_queue/helpers.py`**

```
1→"""Action computation engine used by narrative query generation."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from collections.abc import Callable
     6→from typing import Any
     7→
     8→from desloppify.intelligence.narrative._constants import DETECTOR_TOOLS
     9→from desloppify.intelligence.narrative.action_models import ActionContext, ActionItem
    10→from desloppify.languages import get_lang
    11→from desloppify.scoring import (
    12→    compute_score_impact,
    13→    get_dimension_for_detector,
    14→    merge_potentials,
    15→)
    16→from desloppify.state import StateModel
    17→
    18→
    19→def supported_fixers(state: StateModel, lang: str | None) -> set[str] | None:
    20→    """Return supported fixers for the active language, or None when unknown."""
    21→    if not lang:
    22→        return None
    23→
    24→    capabilities = state.get("lang_capabilities", {}).get(lang, {})
    25→    fixers = capabilities.get("fixers")
    26→    if isinstance(fixers, list):
    27→        return {fixer for fixer in fixers if isinstance(fixer, str)}
    28→
    29→    try:
    30→        return set(get_lang(lang).fixers.keys())
    31→    except (ImportError, ValueError):
    32→        return None
    33→
    34→
    35→def _impact_calculator(
    36→    dimension_scores: dict[str, dict[str, Any]],
    37→    state: StateModel,
    38→) -> Callable[[str, int], float]:
    39→    """Build an impact estimator closure keyed by detector and count."""
    40→    merged_potentials = merge_potentials(state.get("potentials", {}))
    41→    if not merged_potentials or not dimension_scores:
    42→        return lambda _detector, _count: 0.0
    43→
    44→    scoring_view = {
    45→        name: {
    46→            "score": values["score"],
    47→            "tier": values.get("tier", 3),
    48→            "detectors": values.get("detectors", {}),
    49→        }
    50→        for name, values in dimension_scores.items()
    51→    }
    52→
    53→    def _impact(detector: str, count: int) -> float:
    54→        return compute_score_impact(
    55→            scoring_view, merged_potentials, detector, count
    56→        )
    57→
    58→    return _impact
    59→
    60→
    61→def _dimension_name(detector: str) -> str:
    62→    """Resolve user-facing dimension name for a detector."""
    63→    dimension = get_dimension_for_detector(detector)
    64→    return dimension.name if dimension else "Unknown"
    65→
    66→
    67→def _fixer_has_applicable_findings(
    68→    state: StateModel, detector: str, fixer_name: str
    69→) -> bool:
    70→    """For the smells detector, verify the fixer has matching open findings.
    71→
    72→    The smells detector aggregates many smell types but each fixer only handles
    73→    one sub-type (e.g. ``dead-useeffect`` only fixes ``dead_useeffect`` smells).
    74→    Without this check, a React-specific fixer can be suggested to projects that
    75→    have no React code at all, producing a confusing "Found 0 candidates" result.
    76→
    77→    For all other detectors the fixer is considered universally applicable.
    78→    """
    79→    if detector != "smells":
    80→        return True
    81→    smell_id = fixer_name.replace("-", "_")
    82→    return any(
    83→        f.get("status") == "open"
    84→        and f.get("detector") == "smells"
    85→        and f.get("detail", {}).get("smell_id") == smell_id
    86→        for f in state.get("findings", {}).values()
    87→    )
    88→
    89→
    90→def _append_auto_fix_actions(
    91→    actions: list[ActionItem],
    92→    by_detector: dict[str, int],
    93→    supported: set[str] | None,
    94→    impact_for: Callable[[str, int], float],
    95→    state: StateModel,
    96→) -> None:
    97→    """Append auto-fix/manual-fix actions for detectors with auto fixers."""
    98→    for detector, tool_info in DETECTOR_TOOLS.items():
    99→        if tool_info["action_type"] != "auto_fix":
   100→            continue
   101→        count = by_detector.get(detector, 0)
   102→        if count == 0:
   103→            continue
   104→
   105→        impact = round(impact_for(detector, count), 1)
   106→        available_fixers = [
   107→            fixer
   108→            for fixer in tool_info["fixers"]
   109→            if (supported is None or fixer in supported)
   110→            and _fixer_has_applicable_findings(state, detector, fixer)
   111→        ]
   112→        if not available_fixers:
   113→            actions.append(
   114→                {
   115→                    "type": "manual_fix",
   116→                    "detector": detector,
   117→                    "count": count,
   118→                    "description": (
   119→                        f"{count} {detector} findings — inspect with "
   120→                        f"`desloppify next` and fix manually"
   121→                    ),
   122→                    "command": f"desloppify show {detector} --status open",
   123→                    "impact": impact,
   124→                    "dimension": _dimension_name(detector),
   125→                }
   126→            )
   127→            continue
   128→
   129→        fixer = available_fixers[0]
   130→        actions.append(
   131→            {
   132→                "type": "auto_fix",
   133→                "detector": detector,
   134→                "count": count,
   135→                "description": (
   136→                    f"{count} {detector} findings — run "
   137→                    f"`desloppify fix {fixer} --dry-run` to preview, then apply"
   138→                ),
   139→                "command": f"desloppify fix {fixer} --dry-run",
   140→                "impact": impact,
   141→                "dimension": _dimension_name(detector),
   142→            }
   143→        )
   144→
   145→
   146→def _append_reorganize_actions(
   147→    actions: list[ActionItem],
   148→    by_detector: dict[str, int],
   149→    impact_for: Callable[[str, int], float],
   150→) -> None:
   151→    """Append structure/move oriented actions."""
   152→    for detector, tool_info in DETECTOR_TOOLS.items():
   153→        if tool_info["action_type"] != "reorganize":
   154→            continue
   155→        count = by_detector.get(detector, 0)
   156→        if count == 0:
   157→            continue
   158→
   159→        guidance = tool_info.get("guidance", "restructure with move")
   160→        actions.append(
   161→            {
   162→                "type": "reorganize",
   163→                "detector": detector,
   164→                "count": count,
   165→                "description": f"{count} {detector} findings — {guidance}",
   166→                "command": f"desloppify show {detector} --status open",
   167→                "impact": round(impact_for(detector, count), 1),
   168→                "dimension": _dimension_name(detector),
   169→            }
   170→        )
   171→
   172→
   173→def _build_refactor_entry(
   174→    detector: str,
   175→    tool_info: dict[str, Any],
   176→    count: int,
   177→    impact_for: Callable[[str, int], float],
   178→) -> ActionItem:
   179→    """Build one refactor/manual action row."""
   180→    guidance = tool_info.get("guidance", "manual fix")
   181→    adjusted_info = {**tool_info, "guidance": guidance}
   182→
   183→    if detector == "subjective_review":
   184→        command = "desloppify fix review"
   185→        description = f"{count} files need design review — run design review with dimension templates"
   186→    elif detector == "review":
   187→        command = "desloppify show review --status open"
   188→        suffix = "s" if count != 1 else ""
   189→        description = (
   190→            f"{count} review finding{suffix} need investigation — "
   191→            "run `desloppify show review --status open` to see them"
   192→        )
   193→        adjusted_info = {**adjusted_info, "action_type": "refactor"}
   194→    else:
   195→        command = f"desloppify show {detector} --status open"
   196→        description = f"{count} {detector} findings — {guidance}"
   197→
   198→    return {
   199→        "type": adjusted_info["action_type"],
   200→        "detector": detector,
   201→        "count": count,
   202→        "description": description,
   203→        "command": command,
   204→        "impact": round(impact_for(detector, count), 1),
   205→        "dimension": _dimension_name(detector),
   206→    }
   207→
   208→
   209→def _append_refactor_actions(
   210→    actions: list[ActionItem],
   211→    by_detector: dict[str, int],
   212→    impact_for: Callable[[str, int], float],
   213→) -> None:
   214→    """Append refactor/manual actions after auto-fix/reorg buckets."""
   215→    for detector, tool_info in DETECTOR_TOOLS.items():
   216→        if tool_info["action_type"] not in {"refactor", "manual_fix"}:
   217→            continue
   218→        count = by_detector.get(detector, 0)
   219→        if count == 0:
   220→            continue
   221→        actions.append(_build_refactor_entry(detector, tool_info, count, impact_for))
   222→
   223→
   224→def _append_debt_action(actions: list[ActionItem], debt: dict[str, float]) -> None:
   225→    """Append wontfix-debt callout when gap is material."""
   226→    gap = float(debt.get("overall_gap", 0.0) or 0.0)
   227→    if gap <= 2.0:
   228→        return
   229→    actions.append(
   230→        {
   231→            "type": "debt_review",
   232→            "detector": None,
   233→            "description": f"{gap} pts of wontfix debt — review stale decisions",
   234→            "command": "desloppify show --status wontfix",
   235→            "gap": gap,
   236→        }
   237→    )
   238→
   239→
   240→def _assign_priorities(actions: list[ActionItem]) -> list[ActionItem]:
   241→    """Sort and assign sequential priorities."""
   242→    type_order = {
   243→        "issue_queue": 0,
   244→        "auto_fix": 1,
   245→        "reorganize": 2,
   246→        "refactor": 3,
   247→        "manual_fix": 4,
   248→        "debt_review": 5,
   249→    }
   250→    actions.sort(
   251→        key=lambda action: (type_order.get(action["type"], 9), -action.get("impact", 0))
   252→    )
   253→    for index, action in enumerate(actions, start=1):
   254→        action["priority"] = index
   255→    return actions
   256→
   257→
   258→def compute_actions(ctx: ActionContext) -> list[ActionItem]:
   259→    """Compute prioritized action list with tool mapping."""
   260→    actions: list[ActionItem] = []
   261→    impact_for = _impact_calculator(ctx.dimension_scores, ctx.state)
   262→    supported = supported_fixers(ctx.state, ctx.lang)
   263→
   264→    _append_auto_fix_actions(actions, ctx.by_detector, supported, impact_for, ctx.state)
   265→    _append_reorganize_actions(actions, ctx.by_detector, impact_for)
   266→    _append_refactor_actions(actions, ctx.by_detector, impact_for)
   267→    _append_debt_action(actions, ctx.debt)
   268→
   269→    return _assign_priorities(actions)
   270→
```

```
1→"""Headline computation for terminal display."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from ._constants import _history_strict
     6→
     7→
     8→def _compute_headline(
     9→    phase: str,
    10→    dimensions: dict,
    11→    debt: dict,
    12→    milestone: str | None,
    13→    diff: dict | None,
    14→    obj_strict: float | None,
    15→    obj_score: float | None,
    16→    stats: dict,
    17→    history: list[dict],
    18→    open_by_detector: dict | None = None,
    19→) -> str | None:
    20→    """Compute one computed sentence for terminal display."""
    21→    # Security callout prefix — prepended to any headline when security findings exist
    22→    security_count = (open_by_detector or {}).get("security", 0)
    23→    security_prefix = ""
    24→    if security_count > 0:
    25→        s = "s" if security_count != 1 else ""
    26→        security_prefix = f"\u26a0 {security_count} security finding{s} — review before other cleanup. "
    27→
    28→    # Review findings callout — all phases
    29→    review_count = (open_by_detector or {}).get("review", 0)
    30→    review_suffix = ""
    31→    if review_count > 0:
    32→        s = "s" if review_count != 1 else ""
    33→        uninvestigated = (open_by_detector or {}).get("review_uninvestigated", 0)
    34→        if uninvestigated > 0:
    35→            review_suffix = (
    36→                f" ({review_count} review finding{s} \u2014 run `desloppify show review --status open`)"
    37→            )
    38→        else:
    39→            review_suffix = f" ({review_count} review finding{s} pending)"
    40→
    41→    headline = _compute_headline_inner(
    42→        phase, dimensions, debt, milestone, diff, obj_strict, obj_score, stats, history
    43→    )
    44→
    45→    if headline is None and not security_prefix and not review_suffix:
    46→        return None
    47→    parts = security_prefix + (headline or "") + review_suffix
    48→    return parts or None
    49→
    50→
    51→def _compute_headline_inner(
    52→    phase: str,
    53→    dimensions: dict,
    54→    debt: dict,
    55→    milestone: str | None,
    56→    diff: dict | None,
    57→    strict_score: float | None,
    58→    overall_score: float | None,
    59→    stats: dict,
    60→    history: list[dict],
    61→) -> str | None:
    62→    """Compute the base headline (without security prefix)."""
    63→    # Milestone takes priority
    64→    if milestone:
    65→        return milestone
    66→
    67→    # First scan framing
    68→    if phase == "first_scan":
    69→        dims = len(dimensions.get("lowest_dimensions", [])) if dimensions else 0
    70→        open_count = stats.get("open", 0)
    71→        if dims:
    72→            return f"First scan complete. {open_count} open findings across {dims} dimensions."
    73→        return f"First scan complete. {open_count} findings detected."
    74→
    75→    # Regression — acknowledge that drops after fixes are normal
    76→    if phase == "regression":
    77→        prev = _history_strict(history[-2])
    78→        curr = _history_strict(history[-1])
    79→        if prev is not None and curr is not None:
    80→            drop = round(prev - curr, 1)
    81→            return (
    82→                f"Score shifted {drop} pts — this is normal after structural changes. "
    83→                f"Rescan after your next fix to see the real trend."
    84→            )
    85→
    86→    # Stagnation — suggest which dimension to focus on
    87→    if phase == "stagnation":
    88→        if strict_score is not None:
    89→            stuck_scans = min(len(history), 5)
    90→            wontfix = debt.get("wontfix_count", 0)
    91→            # Point to the specific dimension dragging things down
    92→            lowest_dims = dimensions.get("lowest_dimensions", [])
    93→            if lowest_dims:
    94→                dim = lowest_dims[0]
    95→                if wontfix > 0:
    96→                    return (
    97→                        f"Score plateaued at {strict_score:.1f} for {stuck_scans} scans. "
    98→                        f"{dim['name']} ({dim['strict']}%) is where the breakthrough is. "
    99→                        f"{wontfix} wontfix items may also be worth revisiting."
   100→                    )
   101→                return (
   102→                    f"Score plateaued at {strict_score:.1f} for {stuck_scans} scans. "
   103→                    f"{dim['name']} ({dim['strict']}%) is where the breakthrough is."
   104→                )
   105→            if wontfix > 0:
   106→                return (
   107→                    f"Score plateaued at {strict_score:.1f} for {stuck_scans} scans. "
   108→                    f"{wontfix} wontfix items — revisit?"
   109→                )
   110→            return (
   111→                f"Score plateaued at {strict_score:.1f} for {stuck_scans} scans. "
   112→                f"Try tackling a different dimension."
   113→            )
   114→
   115→    # Leverage point (lowest dimension with biggest impact)
   116→    lowest = dimensions.get("lowest_dimensions", [])
   117→    if lowest and lowest[0].get("impact", 0) > 0:
   118→        top = lowest[0]
   119→        return (
   120→            f"{top['name']} is your biggest lever: "
   121→            f"{top['issues']} items → +{top['impact']} pts"
   122→        )
   123→
   124→    # Gap callout
   125→    if debt.get("overall_gap", 0) > 5.0:
   126→        gap = debt["overall_gap"]
   127→        worst = debt.get("worst_dimension", "")
   128→        if strict_score is not None and overall_score is not None:
   129→            return (
   130→                f"Strict {strict_score:.1f} vs overall {overall_score:.1f} — "
   131→                f"{gap} pts of wontfix debt, mostly in {worst}"
   132→            )
   133→
   134→    # Maintenance phase
   135→    if phase == "maintenance":
   136→        return (
   137→            f"Strict {strict_score:.1f}/100 — maintenance mode. Watch for regressions."
   138→        )
   139→
   140→    # Middle grind fallback — point toward next item
   141→    if phase == "middle_grind":
   142→        open_count = stats.get("open", 0)
   143→        if lowest:
   144→            top = lowest[0]
   145→            return (
   146→                f"{open_count} findings open. {top['name']} ({top['strict']}%) "
   147→                f"needs attention — run `desloppify next` to start."
   148→            )
   149→        if open_count > 0:
   150→            return f"{open_count} findings open. Run `desloppify next` for the highest-priority item."
   151→
   152→    # Early momentum fallback — celebrate trajectory
   153→    if phase == "early_momentum" and strict_score is not None:
   154→        open_count = stats.get("open", 0)
   155→        return f"Score {strict_score:.1f}/100 with {open_count} findings open. Keep the momentum going."
   156→
   157→    return None
   158→
```
