```
   121→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   122→# build_show_payload
   123→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   124→
   125→
   126→class TestBuildShowPayload:
   127→    """build_show_payload produces structured JSON for query and --output."""
   128→
   129→    def _make_finding(
   130→        self, fid, *, file="a.ts", detector="unused", tier=2, confidence="high"
   131→    ):
   132→        return {
   133→            "id": fid,
   134→            "file": file,
   135→            "detector": detector,
   136→            "tier": tier,
   137→            "confidence": confidence,
   138→            "summary": f"Finding {fid}",
   139→            "detail": {},
   140→        }
   141→
   142→    def test_empty_matches(self):
   143→        result = build_show_payload([], "*.ts", "open")
   144→        assert result["total"] == 0
   145→        assert result["query"] == "*.ts"
   146→        assert result["status_filter"] == "open"
   147→        assert result["summary"]["files"] == 0
   148→        assert result["by_file"] == {}
   149→
   150→
   151→class TestSuppressedMatchEstimate:
   152→    def _make_finding(self, fid, *, file="a.ts", detector="unused",
   153→                      tier=2, confidence="high"):
   154→        return {
   155→            "id": fid, "file": file, "detector": detector,
   156→            "tier": tier, "confidence": confidence,
   157→            "summary": f"Finding {fid}", "detail": {},
   158→        }
   159→
   160→    def test_detector_name(self):
   161→        assert suppressed_match_estimate("smells", {"smells": 4}) == 4
   162→
   163→    def test_detector_prefix_pattern(self):
   164→        assert suppressed_match_estimate("smells::*::x", {"smells": 7}) == 7
   165→
   166→    def test_unknown_pattern_returns_zero(self):
   167→        assert suppressed_match_estimate("nope", {"smells": 3}) == 0
   168→
   169→    def test_single_finding(self):
   170→        findings = [self._make_finding("unused::a.ts::foo")]
   171→        result = build_show_payload(findings, "a.ts", "open")
   172→        assert result["total"] == 1
   173→        assert result["summary"]["files"] == 1
   174→        assert result["summary"]["by_tier"] == {"T2": 1}
   175→        assert result["summary"]["by_detector"] == {"unused": 1}
   176→        assert "a.ts" in result["by_file"]
   177→        assert len(result["by_file"]["a.ts"]) == 1
   178→
   179→    def test_multiple_files_and_detectors(self):
   180→        findings = [
   181→            self._make_finding(
   182→                "unused::a.ts::foo", file="a.ts", detector="unused", tier=2
   183→            ),
   184→            self._make_finding(
   185→                "smells::b.ts::bar", file="b.ts", detector="smells", tier=3
   186→            ),
   187→            self._make_finding(
   188→                "unused::a.ts::baz", file="a.ts", detector="unused", tier=2
   189→            ),
   190→        ]
   191→        result = build_show_payload(findings, "*", "open")
   192→        assert result["total"] == 3
   193→        assert result["summary"]["files"] == 2
   194→        assert result["summary"]["by_tier"] == {"T2": 2, "T3": 1}
   195→        assert result["summary"]["by_detector"]["unused"] == 2
   196→        assert result["summary"]["by_detector"]["smells"] == 1
   197→
   198→    def test_by_file_sorted_by_count_descending(self):
   199→        findings = [
   200→            self._make_finding("a1", file="a.ts"),
   201→            self._make_finding("a2", file="a.ts"),
   202→            self._make_finding("a3", file="a.ts"),
   203→            self._make_finding("b1", file="b.ts"),
   204→        ]
   205→        result = build_show_payload(findings, "*", "open")
   206→        files = list(result["by_file"].keys())
   207→        # a.ts has 3 findings, b.ts has 1 -- a.ts should come first
   208→        assert files[0] == "a.ts"
   209→
   210→    def test_by_detector_sorted_by_count_descending(self):
   211→        findings = [
   212→            self._make_finding("a1", detector="alpha"),
   213→            self._make_finding("a2", detector="alpha"),
   214→            self._make_finding("b1", detector="beta"),
   215→        ]
   216→        result = build_show_payload(findings, "*", "open")
   217→        dets = list(result["summary"]["by_detector"].keys())
   218→        assert dets[0] == "alpha"
   219→
   220→    def test_payload_includes_hidden_metadata_when_provided(self):
   221→        findings = [self._make_finding("a1", detector="alpha")]
   222→        result = build_show_payload(
   223→            findings,
   224→            "*",
   225→            "open",
   226→            meta=ShowPayloadMeta(
   227→                total_matches=4,
   228→                hidden_by_detector={"alpha": 3},
   229→                noise_budget=1,
   230→                global_noise_budget=2,
   231→            ),
   232→        )
   233→        assert result["total"] == 1
   234→        assert result["total_matching"] == 4
   235→        assert result["hidden"]["total"] == 3
   236→        assert result["hidden"]["by_detector"] == {"alpha": 3}
   237→        assert result["noise_budget"] == 1
   238→        assert result["noise_global_budget"] == 2

   319→    def test_show_uses_shared_queue_for_pattern_filter(self, monkeypatch, capsys):

   322→            state={"last_scan": "2026-01-01", "findings": {}, "scan_path": "src"},
   323→        )
   324→        calls = []
   325→        monkeypatch.setattr(show_cmd_mod, "write_query", lambda _payload: None)
   326→
   327→        def fake_queue(_state, **kwargs):
   328→            calls.append(kwargs)
   329→            return {

   333→                        "kind": "finding",
   334→                        "detector": "smells",
   335→                        "file": "src/a.py",
   336→                        "tier": 3,

   338→                        "summary": "X",

   341→                    }
   342→                ]
   343→            }
   344→
   345→        monkeypatch.setattr(show_scope_mod, "build_work_queue", fake_queue)
   346→        args = SimpleNamespace(
   347→            pattern="src/a.py",
   348→            status="open",
   349→            chronic=False,
   350→            code=False,
   351→            top=20,
   352→            output=None,
   353→            lang=None,
   354→            path=".",
   355→        )
   356→        cmd_show(args)
   357→        out = capsys.readouterr().out
   358→        assert calls
   359→        queue_options = calls[0]["options"]
   360→        assert queue_options.scope == "src/a.py"
   361→        assert queue_options.status == "open"
   362→        assert "1 open findings matching 'src/a.py'" in out

   364→    def test_show_chronic_forces_open_status(self, monkeypatch):
```

```
    56→            )

    61→def cmd_next(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    62→    """Show next highest-priority queue items."""
    63→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
    64→    state = runtime.state
    65→    config = runtime.config
    66→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    67→        return
    68→
    69→    stale_warning = check_tool_staleness(state)
    70→    if stale_warning:

    73→    if skill_warning:

    75→
    76→    _get_items(args, state, config)

    79→def _get_items(args, state: dict, config: dict) -> None:
    80→    tier = getattr(args, "tier", None)

    82→    scope = getattr(args, "scope", None)
    83→    status = getattr(args, "status", "open")

    90→
    91→    queue = build_work_queue(

   102→            explain=explain,

   113→
   114→    payload = next_output_mod.build_query_payload(

   116→    )
   117→    payload["overall_score"] = state_mod.get_overall_score(state)
   118→    payload["objective_score"] = state_mod.get_objective_score(state)
   119→    payload["strict_score"] = state_mod.get_strict_score(state)
   120→    payload["scorecard_dimensions"] = scorecard_dimensions_payload(

   122→        dim_scores=state.get("dimension_scores", {}),

   124→    payload["subjective_measures"] = [

   127→    write_query(payload)
```

```
1→"""Scorecard dimension row helpers for the engine/planning layer.
     2→
     3→Provides ``scorecard_dimension_rows`` without importing app-layer modules.
     4→The app scorecard surface re-exports this so external call-sites are unaffected.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→from desloppify.scoring import DIMENSIONS
    10→
    11→
    12→def scorecard_dimension_rows(
    13→    state: dict,
    14→    *,
    15→    dim_scores: dict | None = None,
    16→) -> list[tuple[str, dict]]:
    17→    """Return scorecard rows using canonical dimension ordering.
    18→
    19→    Tries ``prepare_scorecard_dimensions`` from the app layer when available
    20→    (deferred import to avoid hard engine -> app dependency), then falls back
    21→    to a simple mechanical-dimension listing.
    22→    """
    23→    if dim_scores is None:
    24→        dim_scores = (
    25→            state.get("dimension_scores", {}) if isinstance(state, dict) else {}
    26→        )
    27→        projected_state = state
    28→    else:
    29→        projected_state = dict(state)
    30→        projected_state["dimension_scores"] = dim_scores
    31→
    32→    # Try the full app-layer scorecard projection when it can be imported.
    33→    try:
    34→        from desloppify.app.output.scorecard_parts.dimensions import (
    35→            prepare_scorecard_dimensions,
    36→        )
    37→
    38→        rows = prepare_scorecard_dimensions(projected_state)
    39→        if rows:
    40→            return rows
    41→    except ImportError:
    42→        pass
    43→
    44→    # Fallback for synthetic/unit-test states without full scorecard context.
    45→    fallback_dim_scores = dim_scores or {}
    46→    if not isinstance(fallback_dim_scores, dict):
    47→        return []
    48→
    49→    mechanical_names = [dimension.name for dimension in DIMENSIONS]
    50→    fallback_rows: list[tuple[str, dict]] = []
    51→    for name in mechanical_names:
    52→        data = fallback_dim_scores.get(name)
    53→        if isinstance(data, dict):
    54→            fallback_rows.append((name, data))
    55→    fallback_rows.extend(
    56→        sorted(
    57→            [
    58→                (name, data)
    59→                for name, data in fallback_dim_scores.items()
    60→                if name not in mechanical_names and isinstance(data, dict)
    61→            ],
    62→            key=lambda item: item[0].lower(),
    63→        )
    64→    )
    65→    return fallback_rows
    66→
```

## Implementation Plan: Subjective Guidance System Consolidation

### Overview

Four coordinated changes that consolidate subjective quality guidance into a coherent flow: (1) a `show subjective` dashboard as the single entry point, (2) collapsed nudges in `next` that point to that dashboard, (3) consistent action engine suggestions, and (4) reordered workflow guide.

---

### Change 1: `show subjective` Dashboard

**Goal**: Add `show subjective` as a special keyword (like `show concerns`) that renders a unified subjective quality dashboard and writes structured data to query.json.

**File to modify**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/show/cmd.py`

**Insertion point**: After the `concerns` check at line 168-171, add a new block:

```python
if pattern_raw and pattern_raw.strip().lower() == "subjective":
    lang = resolve_lang(args)
    _show_subjective(state, runtime, lang.name if lang else None, config)
    return
```

**New function `_show_subjective`** (same file, near `_show_concerns`):

This function gathers data from four existing sources and renders four sections:

**Section 1: Dimension scores table**
- Source: `scorecard_subjective_entries(state, dim_scores=dim_scores)` from `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/planning/scorecard_projection.py`
- Also: `unassessed_subjective_dimensions(dim_scores)` from `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/integrity.py`
- Render each subjective dimension with its strict score, status (assessed/placeholder/stale), and open review finding count per dimension.
- For each dimension entry, cross-reference open review findings by checking if their `detail.dimension` or finding ID contains the dimension key.

```python
def _show_subjective(
    state: dict,
    runtime,
    lang_name: str | None,
    config: dict,
) -> None:
    from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.score import target_strict_score_from_config
    from desloppify.engine.planning.scorecard_projection import scorecard_subjective_entries
    from desloppify.intelligence.integrity import (
        subjective_review_open_breakdown,
        unassessed_subjective_dimensions,
    )

    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {}) or {}
    target = target_strict_score_from_config(config, fallback=95.0)
    findings = state.get("findings", {})
    scoped = state_mod.path_scoped_findings(findings, state.get("scan_path"))

    # Gather data
    subjective_entries = scorecard_subjective_entries(state, dim_scores=dim_scores)
    unassessed = unassessed_subjective_dimensions(dim_scores)
    coverage_open, coverage_reasons, holistic_reasons = subjective_review_open_breakdown(scoped)

    # Open review findings (detector=review, status=open)
    open_review = [
        f for f in scoped.values()
        if f.get("status") == "open" and f.get("detector") == "review"
    ]

    # --- Render ---
    print(colorize("\n  Subjective Quality Dashboard", "bold"))
    print(colorize("  " + "-" * 40, "dim"))

    # Section 1: Dimensions
    if subjective_entries:
        print(colorize("\n  Dimensions:", "cyan"))
        for entry in subjective_entries:
            name = entry.get("name", "unknown")
            if entry.get("placeholder"):
                print(f"    {name:<30} [unassessed]")
            else:
                strict = float(entry.get("strict", entry.get("score", 100.0)))
                status_color = "green" if strict >= target else "yellow"
                stale_tag = " (stale)" if entry.get("stale") else ""
                # Count review findings for this dimension
                dim_keys = entry.get("cli_keys", [])
                review_count = sum(
                    1 for f in open_review
                    if any(k in f.get("id", "") or k in str(f.get("detail", {}).get("dimension", ""))
                           for k in dim_keys)
                )
                review_suffix = f"  ({review_count} review findings open)" if review_count else ""
                print(
                    f"    {name:<30} "
                    + colorize(f"{strict:.1f}%", status_color)
                    + colorize(f"{stale_tag}{review_suffix}", "dim")
                )

    # Section 2: Open review findings
    if open_review:
        print(colorize(f"\n  Open Review Findings ({len(open_review)}):", "cyan"))
        for i, f in enumerate(open_review[:10], 1):
            conf = f.get("confidence", "medium")
            print(f"    [{i}] [{conf}] {f.get('summary', '')}")
            print(colorize(f"        {f.get('id', '')}", "dim"))
        if len(open_review) > 10:
            print(colorize(f"    ... and {len(open_review) - 10} more", "dim"))
    else:
        print(colorize("\n  No open review findings.", "dim"))

    # Section 3: Coverage debt
    if coverage_open > 0:
        parts = []
        if coverage_reasons.get("changed", 0): parts.append(f"{coverage_reasons['changed']} changed")
        if coverage_reasons.get("unreviewed", 0): parts.append(f"{coverage_reasons['unreviewed']} unreviewed")
        reason_text = ", ".join(parts) if parts else "stale/unreviewed"
        suffix = "file" if coverage_open == 1 else "files"
        print(colorize(f"\n  Coverage Debt: {coverage_open} {suffix} ({reason_text})", "yellow"))
    else:
        print(colorize("\n  No coverage debt.", "dim"))

    # Section 4: Actions
    print(colorize("\n  Actions:", "cyan"))
    if open_review:
        print("    Investigate findings:   desloppify show <finding-id>")
        print('    Resolve a finding:      desloppify resolve fixed "<id>" --note "..."')
    if unassessed or coverage_open > 0:
        print("    Run new review:         desloppify review --prepare")
    print("    Triage coverage:        desloppify show subjective_review --status open")
    print("    Check progress:         desloppify status")
    print()

    # Write query.json payload
    narrative = compute_narrative(
        state, context=NarrativeContext(lang=lang_name, command="show")
    )
    write_query({
        "command": "show",
        "action": "subjective_dashboard",
        "dimensions": [
            {
                "name": e.get("name", ""),
                "score": float(e.get("score", 0.0)),
                "strict": float(e.get("strict", e.get("score", 0.0))),
                "placeholder": bool(e.get("placeholder")),
                "stale": bool(e.get("stale")),
                "cli_keys": list(e.get("cli_keys", [])),
            }
            for e in subjective_entries
        ],
        "open_review_findings": [
            {
                "id": f.get("id", ""),
                "confidence": f.get("confidence", "medium"),
                "summary": f.get("summary", ""),
            }
            for f in open_review
        ],
        "coverage_debt": {
            "total": coverage_open,
            "reasons": dict(coverage_reasons),
        },
        "unassessed_dimensions": unassessed,
        "target_strict_score": target,
        "narrative": narrative,
    })
```

## Complete Implementation Plan

### Change 1: `show subjective` Dashboard

**Files to modify:**

1. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/show/cmd.py`**

   **Imports to add** (top of file):
   - `from desloppify.state import save_state, path_scoped_findings` (expand existing import)
   - The new function will use deferred imports for `scorecard_subjective_entries`, `subjective_review_open_breakdown`, `unassessed_subjective_dimensions`, and `target_strict_score_from_config` since they are already available from modules that `render.py` already imports.

   **New function** `_show_subjective(state, runtime, lang_name, config)` placed between `_show_concerns` and `cmd_show`:
   - Gather `dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {}) or {}`
   - Call `scorecard_subjective_entries(state, dim_scores=dim_scores)` for dimension list
   - Call `unassessed_subjective_dimensions(dim_scores)` for gap detection
   - Call `path_scoped_findings(findings, scan_path)` to scope findings
   - Call `subjective_review_open_breakdown(scoped)` for coverage debt
   - Filter `scoped` for `detector == "review" and status == "open"` for review findings

   **Render four sections** (as detailed in pseudocode above):
   1. Dimensions table -- scored, unassessed, stale
   2. Open review findings list (capped at 10)
   3. Coverage debt summary
   4. Action commands

   **Write query.json** with structured payload containing:
   ```json
   {
     "command": "show",
     "action": "subjective_dashboard",
     "dimensions": [...],
     "open_review_findings": [...],
     "coverage_debt": {"total": N, "reasons": {...}},
     "unassessed_dimensions": [...],
     "target_strict_score": 95.0,
     "narrative": {...}
   }
   ```

   **Dispatch in `cmd_show`**: Insert after line 171, before the numeric shorthand check:
   ```python
   if pattern_raw and pattern_raw.strip().lower() == "subjective":
       lang = resolve_lang(args)
       _show_subjective(state, runtime, lang.name if lang else None, config)
       return
   ```

   **Design decision: review finding per-dimension cross-referencing.** Review findings have `detail.dimension_key` or encode the dimension in the finding ID (e.g., `review::src/foo.ts::naming_quality::processData`). For the dimension-level review count, iterate open review findings and match by checking if any of the entry's `cli_keys` appears in the finding's ID (splitting on `::`). This is a best-effort linkage -- if a finding cannot be matched, it still appears in the full listing.

```python
# --- Collapsed subjective summary (blocks 2-6 replaced) ---

low_dims = len(followup.low_assessed)
unassessed_count = len(unassessed_subjective)
open_review = [
    f for f in findings_scoped.values()
    if f.get("status") == "open" and f.get("detector") == "review"
]
open_review_count = len(open_review)

parts: list[str] = []
if low_dims:

if unassessed_count:
    parts.append(f"{unassessed_count} unassessed")
if open_review_count:

if coverage_open > 0:
    parts.append(f"{coverage_open} file{'s' if coverage_open != 1 else ''} need review")

# Always show integrity notice lines (penalty/warn) since they represent serious issues
for style, message in followup.integrity_lines:
    print(colorize(f"\n  {message}", style))

if parts:
    print(colorize(f"\n  Subjective: {', '.join(parts)}.", "cyan"))
    print(colorize("  Run `desloppify show subjective` for details.", "dim"))
```

This reduces 15+ lines of output to 2 lines (plus any integrity penalty lines which are critical warnings and should remain). The `print_subjective_followup` import can be removed from this file.

**Key design decision**: Integrity penalty/warn lines (`followup.integrity_lines`) are preserved because they represent anti-gaming safeguards that must remain visible. The rest is collapsed.

**Impact on imports**: Remove `print_subjective_followup` from the import at line 6 (it's imported from `desloppify.app.commands.helpers.subjective`). The function can remain in `__all__` of that module since `show/render.py` still uses it.

### Change 4: Workflow Guide Reorder

**File to modify**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_reporting_text.py`

**Current order**: Review findings -> Auto-fixers -> Manual fixes -> Rescan -> Reset subjective -> Check progress

**New order**:

```python
def build_workflow_guide(attest_example: str) -> str:
    return dedent(
        f"""
        ## Workflow Guide

        1. **Run auto-fixers** (if available): `desloppify fix <fixer> --dry-run` to preview, then apply
        2. **Manual fixes**: `desloppify next` — highest-priority item. Fix it, then:
           `desloppify resolve fixed "<id>" --note "<what you did>" --attest "{attest_example}"`
           Required attestation keywords: 'I have actually' and 'not gaming'.
        3. **Rescan**: `desloppify scan --path <path>` — verify improvements, catch cascading effects
        4. **Subjective review**: `desloppify review --prepare` → import results → `desloppify show subjective`
        5. **Reset subjective baseline when needed**:
           `desloppify scan --path <path> --reset-subjective` (then run a fresh review/import cycle)
        6. **Check progress**: `desloppify status` — dimension scores dashboard

        ### Decision Guide
        - **Tackle**: T1/T2 (high impact), auto-fixable, security findings
        - **Consider skipping**: T4 low-confidence, test/config zone findings (lower impact)
        - **Wontfix**: Intentional patterns, false positives →
          `desloppify resolve wontfix "<id>" --note "<why>" --attest "{attest_example}"`
        - **Batch wontfix**: Multiple intentional patterns →
          `desloppify resolve wontfix "<detector>::*::<category>" --note "<why>" --attest "{attest_example}"`

        ### Understanding Dimensions
        - **Mechanical** (File health, Code quality, etc.): Fix code → rescan
        - **Subjective** (Naming quality, Logic clarity, etc.): Address review findings → re-review
        - **Health vs Strict**: Health ignores wontfix; Strict penalizes it. Focus on Strict.
        """
    ).strip()
```

Key changes:
- Step 1 is now auto-fixers (universally applicable when findings exist)
- Step 4 is the subjective review workflow, contextualized as a full cycle, and points to `show subjective` for triage
- The old "Review findings first" step is removed as a standalone step; reviewing is part of step 4

**Snapshot impact**: The scan snapshot at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/snapshots/cli_smoke/scan.txt` contains the workflow guide output and will need regeneration.

### Test Strategy

#### Tests for Change 1 (`show subjective`)

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/commands/test_cmd_show.py`

**New test class**: `TestShowSubjective`

Follow the pattern of `TestCmdShowBackendIntegration` (which patches `command_runtime`, `resolve_lang`, `compute_narrative`, etc.):

```python
class TestShowSubjective:
    def _patch_common(self, monkeypatch, *, state, config=None):
        monkeypatch.setattr(
            show_cmd_mod,
            "command_runtime",
            lambda _args: CommandRuntime(
                config=config or {},
                state=state,
                state_path="/tmp/fake-state.json",
            ),
        )
        monkeypatch.setattr(show_cmd_mod, "check_tool_staleness", lambda _state: None)
        monkeypatch.setattr(show_cmd_mod, "compute_narrative", lambda *a, **k: {})
        monkeypatch.setattr(show_cmd_mod, "resolve_lang", lambda _args: None)
        monkeypatch.setattr(show_cmd_mod, "write_query", lambda _payload: None)

    def test_show_subjective_renders_dimension_scores(self, monkeypatch, capsys):
        """Dashboard shows assessed dimension scores."""
        self._patch_common(monkeypatch, state={
            "last_scan": "2026-01-01",
            "findings": {},
            "scan_path": ".",
            "dimension_scores": {
                "Naming quality": {
                    "score": 72.5, "strict": 72.5, "issues": 3,
                    "detectors": {"subjective_assessment": {}},
                },
            },
        })
        args = SimpleNamespace(pattern="subjective", lang=None, path=".", notes=None)
        cmd_show(args)
        out = capsys.readouterr().out
        assert "Subjective Quality Dashboard" in out

    def test_show_subjective_writes_query_json(self, monkeypatch):
```

**New test**: Verify that when multiple subjective issues coexist, only the collapsed summary appears (not the verbose blocks):
```python
def test_subjective_nudges_collapsed_to_single_summary(self, monkeypatch, capsys):
    """When multiple subjective issues exist, only one summary line + pointer."""
    _patch_common(monkeypatch, state={
        "findings": {
            "subjective_review::src/a.py::changed": {
                "id": "subjective_review::src/a.py::changed",
                "detector": "subjective_review",
                "file": "src/a.py", "tier": 4,
                "confidence": "medium", "status": "open",
                "summary": "File changed", "detail": {"reason": "changed"},
            },
            "review::src/b.ts::naming_quality::foo": {
                "id": "review::src/b.ts::naming_quality::foo",
                "detector": "review", "file": "src/b.ts",
                "tier": 4, "confidence": "high", "status": "open",
                "summary": "foo is too vague", "detail": {},
            },
        },
        "dimension_scores": {
            "Naming quality": {
                "score": 80.0, "strict": 80.0, "issues": 2,
                "detectors": {"subjective_assessment": {}},
            },
        },
        "overall_score": 85.0, "objective_score": 92.0,
        "strict_score": 85.0, "scan_path": ".",
    })
    # ... (patch build_work_queue)
    cmd_next(_args())
    out = capsys.readouterr().out
    # Collapsed summary
    assert "Subjective:" in out
    assert "below target" in out
    assert "review finding" in out
    assert "need review" in out
    assert "show subjective" in out
    # OLD verbose blocks should NOT appear
    assert "Subjective quality (<" not in out
    assert "Subjective coverage debt:" not in out
    assert "Subjective integrity gap:" not in out
    assert "Subjective bottleneck:" not in out
```

```python
def test_subjective_review_action_suggests_review_prepare(self, empty_state):
    """The action engine should suggest review --prepare, not fix review."""
    result = _compute_actions(
        ActionContext(
            by_detector={"subjective_review": 20},
            dimension_scores={},
            state=empty_state,
            debt={},
            lang="typescript",
        )
    )
    sr_actions = [a for a in result if a.get("detector") == "subjective_review"]
    assert sr_actions
    assert sr_actions[0]["command"] == "desloppify review --prepare"
```

#### Tests for Change 4 (workflow guide)

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/commands/test_cmd_scan.py` or a new small test:

```python
def test_workflow_guide_starts_with_auto_fixers():
    from desloppify.app.commands.scan.scan_reporting_text import build_workflow_guide
    guide = build_workflow_guide("I have actually verified this, not gaming")
    lines = guide.split("\n")
    # First numbered step should be auto-fixers
    step1_line = next(l for l in lines if l.strip().startswith("1."))
    assert "auto-fixer" in step1_line.lower() or "fix <fixer>" in step1_line
    # Subjective review should be step 4
    step4_line = next(l for l in lines if l.strip().startswith("4."))
    assert "review --prepare" in step4_line
    assert "show subjective" in step4_line
```

---

### Implementation Sequence

The changes should be implemented in this order due to dependencies:

1. **Change 3** (action engine fix) -- standalone, no dependencies
2. **Change 4** (workflow guide reorder) -- standalone, no dependencies
3. **Change 1** (`show subjective` dashboard) -- standalone, but referenced by Change 2
4. **Change 2** (nudge collapse) -- depends on Change 1 (references `show subjective`)
5. **Snapshot regeneration** -- after all code changes, re-run smoke tests
6. **Test updates** -- update existing tests that assert on old nudge text

Changes 1 and 2 can be done in parallel since the only dependency is the command name `show subjective` (a string literal), but logically Change 1 should land first so the command exists when Change 2 references it.

Changes 3 and 4 are completely independent and can be done first or in parallel.

---

### Potential Challenges

1. **Per-dimension review finding count accuracy**: The cross-referencing between dimension entries and review findings relies on finding IDs containing dimension keys. If some review findings use different ID structures, counts may be inaccurate. Mitigation: This is supplementary information (the total is always correct), and the heuristic matches the existing ID convention `review::<file>::<dimension>::<symbol>`.

2. **Test snapshot regeneration**: Three snapshot files contain `fix review` text. These are generated by running actual CLI commands against fixture state. They need to be regenerated after the action engine change. If the CI pipeline has snapshot comparison, it will fail until regenerated.

3. **Existing test assertions**: At least 6 tests in `test_cmd_next.py` assert on the old verbose nudge format. Each needs to be updated to assert on the collapsed format instead. The new assertions are simpler (just checking for summary terms and the `show subjective` pointer).

4. **`print_subjective_followup` import in render.py**: After Change 2, this import is no longer used in `next_parts/render.py`. However, it's still used in `show/render.py`. The import should be removed from `next_parts/render.py` only, and the `__all__` export kept in `helpers/subjective.py`.

---
